> - A MUN H
> - F BER H

### AUSTRIA
Orders: `A BUD H` · `A GRE H` · `A TRI H` · `A VIE H`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I'm honoring my publicly stated position to all neighbors that I'm holding this fall. My commitment to Russia to support his RUM attack was a tactical probe after the dialogue summary ended, but I never received confirmation from him, and the specific order for GRE is not legal (GRE is not adjacent to RUM). Holding keeps my 5 centers secure, avoids entangling me in the Turkey-Russia fight, and preserves my ability to pivot toward Italy's Med or Germany's flank in 1909 once the board clarifies. Breaking a unilateral offer is less costly than betraying a confirmed deal.

</details>

My convoy route through NTH needs protection from F DEN's potential attack. I'll support F NTH with both F LON and F NWY to keep the convoy safe, while A YOR strikes BEL with French support. This is the decisive move I promised Italy and France.

</details>

## FALL 1908 RETREATS (`F1908R`)

<img src="F1908R.svg" alt="F1908R map" width="700">

### What happened

- **GERMANY**: A BEL retreats → BUR
- **RUSSIA**: A SEV retreats → MOS

### GERMANY
Orders: `A BEL R BUR`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I have one unit to manage this retreat phase: A BEL was dislodged by the English convoy attack supported by France's F ENG. My strategy note called for taking BRE this fall, but that move failed because France supported England into BEL instead of holding as agreed. Now I need to retreat A BEL to a safe adjacent province. BUR is adjacent and empty — I can regroup there and prepare to retake BEL next turn, or support a future push against France or England. PIC is also adjacent but France's A BRE is nearby and I have A PAR in PIC's neighborhood already. BUR gives me the best position to threaten both BEL and the French interior.

</details>

### RUSSIA
Orders: `A SEV R MOS`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> The Austrians betrayed me — they promised support for my retaking of RUM but held instead, letting Turkey take SEV. I'm now down to 3 units (plus the retreating A SEV) against 4 centers about to become 3 after SEV falls. I need to preserve every unit I can from this disaster. A SEV retreating to MOS keeps an army alive in a home center, positioning it to either threaten south toward UKR or defend the north against any opportunistic push from Austria or Germany. In the Winter phase I'll have four units vs three centers and must disband one, likely A UKR (surrounded and cut off), leaving me with A GAL, A MOS, and F STP/NC — a workable core to rebuild around if I can secure peace with at least one of my enemies.

</details>

## WINTER 1910 ADJUSTMENTS (`W1910A`)

<img src="W1910A.svg" alt="W1910A map" width="700">

### What happened

- **FRANCE**: builds A MAR
- **ITALY**: disbands F TUN

### FRANCE
Orders: `A MAR B`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I took SPA in Fall 1910, so I have two supply centers (MAR and SPA) and need to build exactly one unit this winter. MAR is vacant and under my control, giving me a legal build location. Building a fleet at MAR would give me sea power to contest the Western Mediterranean and support future operations against Germany's coastal holdings, but an army at MAR is more useful for land operations against Germany's southern flank in 1911. Given my need to pressure Germany from the south and coordinate with Austria on TYR, an army at MAR is the right choice.

</details>

### ITALY
Orders: `F TUN D`

<details><summary>Reasoning</summary>

> I need to review my supply-center situation and plan winter builds or disbands.
>
> **Supply Center count:** ITALY has 5 centers (NAP, ROM, VEN, TUN, POR). That's a deficit of 1 compared to my 6 units (F TUN, F VEN, F NAP, F ION, A POR, F WES). Wait — I need to recount. My centers: NAP, ROM, VEN, TUN, POR = 5. My units: F TUN, F VEN, F NAP, F ION, A POR, F WES = 6. So I have 1 unit more than my supply count. I must disband 1 unit.
>
> ROM is vacant and is a home center. I could build there if I had surplus, but I have a deficit. So I must disband one unit.
>
> Which unit to disband? Looking forward: I need to take Spain next spring (A POR - SPA, F WES - SPA/SC). After that, I want to push F WES into MAO to threaten Germany. My Med fleets (F ION, F TUN) are also important. F VEN holds the Adriatic with Austria, and F NAP can be useful in the Tyrrhenian. 
>
> The weakest link for future ops is probably F TUN or F NAP. F TUN is isolated in the western Med and less useful for the coming push. F NAP can support into TYS or ION. I'll disband F TUN, keeping my other 5 units to match my 5 centers.
>
> I told Austria and Turkey I'd hold F VEN and F ION. I told England I'd push into MAO in spring after taking Spain. Disbanding F TUN frees up a unit slot without breaking commitments.

</details>
